## Assistant

Created the separate working file: [Iowa-Story-tornadoes-working.csv](E:/GitHub/coyne-week-7-codex-hands-on/analysis/Iowa-Story-tornadoes-working.csv).

It contains all **70 Story County records** listed on [Tornado Path’s Story County page](https://www.tornadopath.com/iowa/story), dated 1882–2025. The CSV includes year, date, intensity, direct and indirect fatalities and injuries with totals, reported and numeric USD property and crop damage, and available track details. I preserved the site’s reported damage values alongside the USD conversions.

The records sum to **1 fatality and 27 injuries**, matching the page’s totals. Three records have no intensity listed. I also added this work to the [AI workflow log](E:/GitHub/coyne-week-7-codex-hands-on/log/ai-workflow-log.md).

## User

Alright let's begin exploring this data within a jupyter notebook, storing both spreadsheets as pandas dataframes. We also don't need any tornado data from before our housing data begins, so in our notebook we can omit any tornadoes that occurred before our first home was built, if there are any.

    {
     "cells": [
      {
       "cell_type": "markdown",
       "metadata": {},
       "source": [
        "# Ames Housing and Story County Tornadoes\n",
        "\n",
        "Load the two working CSV files into pandas DataFrames and explore their time ranges and basic summaries. The tornado DataFrame is filtered to years at or after the earliest home construction year. This notebook reads the source CSVs without changing them."
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "from pathlib import Path\n",
        "import pandas as pd"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Find the project root whether Jupyter starts in the repository root or analysis/.\n",
        "project_root = next(\n",
        "    (candidate for candidate in (Path.cwd(), *Path.cwd().parents)\n",
        "     if (candidate / \"analysis\" / \"AmesHousing-working.csv\").exists()),\n",
        "    None,\n",
        ")\n",
        "if project_root is None:\n",
        "    raise FileNotFoundError(\"Could not find analysis/AmesHousing-working.csv from the current directory.\")\n",
        "\n",
        "housing_path = project_root / \"analysis\" / \"AmesHousing-working.csv\"\n",
        "tornado_path = project_root / \"analysis\" / \"Iowa-Story-tornadoes-working.csv\"\n",
        "print(f\"Project folder: {project_root}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Housing file: {housing_path.name}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornado file: {tornado_path.name}\")"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Keep both source tables as pandas DataFrames.\n",
        "homes_df = pd.read_csv(housing_path)\n",
        "tornadoes_df = pd.read_csv(tornado_path)\n",
        "\n",
        "print(f\"Homes: {homes_df.shape[0]:,} rows × {homes_df.shape[1]} columns\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornado records before filtering: {tornadoes_df.shape[0]:,}\")"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Remove tornado records from before the first home's construction year.\n",
        "homes_df[\"Year Built\"] = pd.to_numeric(homes_df[\"Year Built\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n",
        "tornadoes_df[\"Year\"] = pd.to_numeric(tornadoes_df[\"Year\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n",
        "first_home_year = int(homes_df[\"Year Built\"].min())\n",
        "earliest_tornado_year = int(tornadoes_df[\"Year\"].min())\n",
        "excluded_tornadoes = tornadoes_df.loc[tornadoes_df[\"Year\"] < first_home_year].copy()\n",
        "tornadoes_df = tornadoes_df.loc[tornadoes_df[\"Year\"] >= first_home_year].copy()\n",
        "\n",
        "print(f\"Earliest home construction year: {first_home_year}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Earliest tornado year in the source file: {earliest_tornado_year}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornadoes excluded as too early: {len(excluded_tornadoes)}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornado records retained: {len(tornadoes_df)}\")"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Check the columns and the time spans now represented in each DataFrame.\n",
        "display(homes_df[[\"Year Built\", \"Yr Sold\", \"SalePrice\", \"Total Bsmt SF\"]].describe())\n",
        "display(tornadoes_df[[\"Year\", \"FatalitiesTotal\", \"InjuriesTotal\", \"PropertyDamageUSD\", \"CropDamageUSD\"]].describe())\n",
        "\n",
        "print(\"Tornado intensity values (including missing ratings):\")\n",
        "display(tornadoes_df[\"Intensity\"].value_counts(dropna=False).sort_index())"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Initial housing summary by whether a recorded basement is present.\n",
        "homes_df[\"Total Bsmt SF\"] = pd.to_numeric(homes_df[\"Total Bsmt SF\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n",
        "homes_df[\"Has Basement\"] = homes_df[\"Total Bsmt SF\"].fillna(0).gt(0)\n",
        "basement_price_summary = (\n",
        "    homes_df.groupby(\"Has Basement\")[\"SalePrice\"]\n",
        "    .agg(Homes=\"count\", MeanSalePrice=\"mean\", MedianSalePrice=\"median\")\n",
        "    .rename(index={False: \"No recorded basement\", True: \"Basement present\"})\n",
        ")\n",
        "display(basement_price_summary)"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Annual tornado counts and impacts provide a starting point for later comparisons.\n",
        "annual_tornado_summary = (\n",
        "    tornadoes_df.groupby(\"Year\")\n",
        "    .agg(\n",
        "        Tornadoes=(\"EventID\", \"count\"),\n",
        "        Fatalities=(\"FatalitiesTotal\", \"sum\"),\n",
        "        Injuries=(\"InjuriesTotal\", \"sum\"),\n",
        "        PropertyDamageUSD=(\"PropertyDamageUSD\", \"sum\"),\n",
        "        CropDamageUSD=(\"CropDamageUSD\", \"sum\"),\n",
        "    )\n",
        ")\n",
        "display(annual_tornado_summary)"
       ]
      },
      {

       "source": [
        "## Interpretation note\n",
        "\n",
        "A tornado recorded somewhere in Story County during a calendar year does not establish that an individual Ames home was exposed to that tornado. Treat annual summaries as county-level context; track locations and sale timing would be needed for a more specific exposure comparison."
       ]

## Assistant

Created [ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb](E:/GitHub/coyne-week-7-codex-hands-on/analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb). It loads both working CSVs into pandas DataFrames, filters tornadoes earlier than the first home’s construction year, and includes initial summaries of sale prices by basement presence and tornado impacts by year.

The earliest home was built in **1872** and the earliest tornado record is **1882**, so the filter will exclude **zero** tornadoes with the current files. The notebook reads the CSVs without modifying them.

## User

It looks like our tornado dataset is a bit sparse, why dont we expand it to story county and its surrounding counties. so story, hamilton, hardin, marshall, jasper, polk, and boone.

## Assistant

I’ll expand the tornado working data to those seven counties, keeping each event’s county so we can filter or aggregate later. I’ll check the county pages’ record counts and pull their available events from the same source.

          "Project folder: E:\\GitHub\\coyne-week-7-codex-hands-on\n",
          "Housing file: AmesHousing-working.csv\n",
          "Tornado file: Iowa-central-counties-tornadoes-working.csv\n"
         ]
        }
       ],
       "source": [
        "# Find the project root whether Jupyter starts in the repository root or analysis/.\n",
        "project_root = next(\n",
        "    (candidate for candidate in (Path.cwd(), *Path.cwd().parents)\n",
        "     if (candidate / \"analysis\" / \"AmesHousing-working.csv\").exists()),\n",
        "    None,\n",
        ")\n",
        "if project_root is None:\n",
        "    raise FileNotFoundError(\"Could not find analysis/AmesHousing-working.csv from the current directory.\")\n",
        "\n",
        "housing_path = project_root / \"analysis\" / \"AmesHousing-working.csv\"\n",
        "tornado_path = project_root / \"analysis\" / \"Iowa-central-counties-tornadoes-working.csv\"\n",
        "print(f\"Project folder: {project_root}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Housing file: {housing_path.name}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornado file: {tornado_path.name}\")"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": 3,
       "id": "895f7fc8",
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [
        {
         "name": "stdout",
         "output_type": "stream",
         "text": [
          "Homes: 2,884 rows — 82 columns\n",
          "Tornado records before filtering: 435\n"
         ]
        }
       ],
       "source": [
        "# Keep both source tables as pandas DataFrames.\n",
        "homes_df = pd.read_csv(housing_path)\n",
        "tornadoes_df = pd.read_csv(tornado_path)\n",
        "\n",
        "print(f\"Homes: {homes_df.shape[0]:,} rows — {homes_df.shape[1]} columns\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornado records before filtering: {tornadoes_df.shape[0]:,}\")"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": 4,
       "id": "2234c155",
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [
        {
         "name": "stdout",
         "output_type": "stream",
         "text": [
          "Earliest home construction year: 1872\n",
          "Earliest tornado year in the source file: 1860\n",
          "Tornadoes excluded as too early: 1\n",
          "Tornado records retained: 434\n"
         ]
        }
       ],
       "source": [
        "# Remove tornado records from before the first home's construction year.\n",
        "homes_df[\"Year Built\"] = pd.to_numeric(homes_df[\"Year Built\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n",
        "tornadoes_df[\"Year\"] = pd.to_numeric(tornadoes_df[\"Year\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n",
        "first_home_year = int(homes_df[\"Year Built\"].min())\n",
        "earliest_tornado_year = int(tornadoes_df[\"Year\"].min())\n",
        "excluded_tornadoes = tornadoes_df.loc[tornadoes_df[\"Year\"] < first_home_year].copy()\n",
        "tornadoes_df = tornadoes_df.loc[tornadoes_df[\"Year\"] >= first_home_year].copy()\n",
        "\n",
        "print(f\"Earliest home construction year: {first_home_year}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Earliest tornado year in the source file: {earliest_tornado_year}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornadoes excluded as too early: {len(excluded_tornadoes)}\")\n",
        "print(f\"Tornado records retained: {len(tornadoes_df)}\")"
       ]
      },

       ],
       "source": [
        "# Initial housing summary by whether a recorded basement is present.\n",
        "homes_df[\"Total Bsmt SF\"] = pd.to_numeric(homes_df[\"Total Bsmt SF\"], errors=\"coerce\")\n",
        "homes_df[\"Has Basement\"] = homes_df[\"Total Bsmt SF\"].fillna(0).gt(0)\n",
        "basement_price_summary = (\n",
        "    homes_df.groupby(\"Has Basement\")[\"SalePrice\"]\n",
        "    .agg(Homes=\"count\", MeanSalePrice=\"mean\", MedianSalePrice=\"median\")\n",
        "    .rename(index={False: \"No recorded basement\", True: \"Basement present\"})\n",
        ")\n",
        "display(basement_price_summary)"
       ]
      },

       "source": [
        "# Count each county's tornado records across all years retained by the housing-year cutoff.\n",
        "tornado_count_by_county = (\n",
        "    tornadoes_df.groupby(\"County\")[\"EventID\"]\n",
        "    .nunique()\n",
        "    .sort_values(ascending=False)\n",
        "    .rename(\"Tornado Count\")\n",
        "    .to_frame()\n",
        ")\n",
        "display(tornado_count_by_county)"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "markdown",
       "id": "eba6f760",
       "metadata": {},
       "source": [
        "## Interpretation note\n",
        "\n",
        "A tornado recorded in one of these counties during a calendar year does not establish that an individual Ames home was exposed to that tornado. Treat county and annual summaries as regional context; track locations and sale timing would be needed for a more specific exposure comparison."
       ]
      }

      {
       "cell_type": "markdown",
       "metadata": {},
       "source": [
        "# Ames Housing: Initial Summary\n",
        "\n",
        "Start with the working housing dataset in one pandas DataFrame. Year fields are loaded as strings and summarized as categorical values."
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "from pathlib import Path\n",
        "import pandas as pd\n",
        "from IPython.display import display\n",
        "\n",
        "# Keep summary values readable without decimals or scientific notation.\n",
        "pd.options.display.float_format = \"{:,.0f}\".format"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "# Locate the project folder when Jupyter starts in the repository or analysis folder.\n",
        "project_root = next(\n",
        "    (candidate for candidate in (Path.cwd(), *Path.cwd().parents)\n",
        "     if (candidate / \"analysis\" / \"AmesHousing-working.csv\").exists()),\n",
        "    None,\n",
        ")\n",
        "if project_root is None:\n",
        "    raise FileNotFoundError(\"Could not find analysis/AmesHousing-working.csv from the current directory.\")\n",
        "\n",
        "housing_path = project_root / \"analysis\" / \"AmesHousing-working.csv\"\n",
        "year_columns = [\"Year Built\", \"Year Remod/Add\", \"Garage Yr Blt\", \"Yr Sold\"]\n",
        "homes_df = pd.read_csv(\n",
        "    housing_path,\n",
        "    dtype={column: \"string\" for column in year_columns},\n",
        ")\n",
        "\n",
        "print(f\"Loaded {homes_df.shape[0]:,} homes and {homes_df.shape[1]} columns.\")\n",
        "print(\"Year column types:\")\n",
        "display(homes_df[year_columns].dtypes.to_frame(name=\"Data type\"))"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "markdown",
       "metadata": {},
       "source": [
        "## Numeric summary statistics\n",
        "\n",
        "Year columns are strings, so they are excluded from this numeric summary."
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "numeric_summary = homes_df.describe().T\n",
        "display(numeric_summary)"
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "markdown",
       "metadata": {},
       "source": [
        "## Year-field summaries\n",
        "\n",
        "These summaries treat years as strings and report counts, distinct values, most common values, and their frequencies."
       ]
      },
      {
       "cell_type": "code",
       "execution_count": null,
       "metadata": {},
       "outputs": [],
       "source": [
        "year_summary = homes_df[year_columns].describe().T\n",
        "display(year_summary)"
       ]
      }
     ],
     "metadata": {

    Only housing path referenced: from pathlib import Path
    import pandas as pd
    from IPython.display import display
    
    # Keep summary values readable without decimals or scientific notation.
    pd.options.display.float_format = "{:,.0f}".format # Locate the project folder when Jupyter starts in the repository or analysis folder.
    project_root = next(
        (candidate for candidate in (Path.cwd(), *Path.cwd().parents)
         if (candidate / "analysis" / "AmesHousing-working.csv").exists()),
        None,
    )
    if project_root is None:
        raise FileNotFoundError("Could not find analysis/AmesHousing-working.csv from the current directory.")
    
    housing_path = project_root / "analysis" / "AmesHousing-working.csv"
    year_columns = ["Year Built", "Year Remod/Add", "Garage Yr Blt", "Yr Sold"]
    homes_df = pd.read_csv(
        housing_path,
        dtype={column: "string" for column in year_columns},
    )
    
    print(f"Loaded {homes_df.shape[0]:,} homes and {homes_df.shape[1]} columns.")
    print("Year column types:")
    display(homes_df[year_columns].dtypes.to_frame(name="Data type")) numeric_summary = homes_df.describe().T
    display(numeric_summary) year_summary = homes_df[year_columns].describe().T
    display(year_summary)

        "outputs": [],

    +   "source": [
    +    "# Load the expanded seven-county tornado dataset. Keep the year column as strings.\n",
    +    "tornado_path = project_root / \"analysis\" / \"Iowa-central-counties-tornadoes-working.csv\"\n",
    +    "tornadoes_df = pd.read_csv(tornado_path, dtype={\"Year\": \"string\"})\n",
    +    "\n",
    +    "years_of_interest = {str(year) for year in range(2005, 2011)}\n",
    +    "tornadoes_2005_2010_df = tornadoes_df.loc[\n",
    +    "    tornadoes_df[\"Year\"].isin(years_of_interest)\n",
    +    "].copy()\n",
    +    "\n",
    +    "print(f\"Loaded {len(tornadoes_df):,} tornado records across all years.\")\n",
    +    "print(f\"Records from 2005–2010: {len(tornadoes_2005_2010_df):,}\")\n",
    +    "print(\"Years in the selected data:\")\n",
    +    "display(tornadoes_2005_2010_df[\"Year\"].value_counts().sort_index().to_frame(\"Tornado Count\"))"
    +   ]
    +  },
    +  {
    +   "cell_type": "markdown",
    +   "id": "tornado-summary-heading",
    +   "metadata": {},
    +   "source": [
    +    "## Tornado summary statistics, 2005–2010\n",
    +    "\n",
    +    "These summaries use only tornado records in the selected years. County counts and intensity counts show how the records are distributed within that period."
    +   ]
    +  },
    +  {
    +   "cell_type": "code",
    +   "execution_count": null,
    +   "id": "tornado-impact-summary",
    +   "metadata": {},
    +   "outputs": [],
    +   "source": [
    +    "impact_columns = [\n",
    +    "    \"FatalitiesDirect\", \"FatalitiesIndirect\", \"FatalitiesTotal\",\n",
    +    "    \"InjuriesDirect\", \"InjuriesIndirect\", \"InjuriesTotal\",\n",
    +    "    \"PropertyDamageUSD\", \"CropDamageUSD\",\n",
    +    "    \"TornadoLengthMiles\", \"TornadoWidthYards\",\n",
    +    "]\n",
    +    "tornado_impact_summary = tornadoes_2005_2010_df[impact_columns].describe().T\n",
    +    "display(tornado_impact_summary)"
    +   ]
    +  },
    +  {
    +   "cell_type": "code",
    +   "execution_count": null,
    +   "id": "tornado-county-counts",
    +   "metadata": {},
    +   "outputs": [],
    +   "source": [
    +    "tornado_count_by_county = (\n",
    +    "    tornadoes_2005_2010_df.groupby(\"County\")[\"EventID\"]\n",
    +    "    .nunique()\n",
    +    "    .sort_values(ascending=False)\n",
    +    "    .rename(\"Tornado Count\")\n",
    +    "    .to_frame()\n",
    +    ")\n",
    +    "display(tornado_count_by_county)\n",
    +    "\n",
    +    "display(\n",
    +    "    tornadoes_2005_2010_df[\"Intensity\"]\n",
    +    "    .value_counts(dropna=False)\n",
    +    "    .rename_axis(\"Intensity\")\n",
    +    "    .to_frame(\"Tornado Count\")\n",
    +    ")"
    +   ]
       }

## Assistant

Added a yearly sales summary and two side-by-side charts to [ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb](E:/GitHub/coyne-week-7-codex-hands-on/analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb): average sale price and number of sales. The charts use the string-valued `Yr Sold` field, with years sorted chronologically and whole-dollar/whole-number axes. Run the new cells in Jupyter to display them.

Rather than the year grain we should switch to months. that way we can get a better idea of the impact they have, if any, on home sales. can you add a column to the tornado table that matches the Mo Sold column format in the housing table

    --- 0 markdown id=3589221e ---
    # Ames Housing and Tornado Data
    
    The housing data is loaded into `homes_df`, with year fields kept as strings. The expanded tornado data is loaded into `tornadoes_df`; the summaries below focus on records from 2005 through 2010.
    --- 1 code id=6e621053 ---
    from pathlib import Path
    import pandas as pd
    from IPython.display import display
    
    # Keep summary values readable without decimals or scientific notation.
    pd.options.display.float_format = "{:,.0f}".format
    outputs=0, execution=1
    --- 2 code id=35489faa ---
    # Locate the project folder when Jupyter starts in the repository or analysis folder.
    project_root = next(
        (candidate for candidate in (Path.cwd(), *Path.cwd().parents)
         if (candidate / "analysis" / "AmesHousing-working.csv").exists()),
        None,
    )
    if project_root is None:
        raise FileNotFoundError("Could not find analysis/AmesHousing-working.csv from the current directory.")
    
    housing_path = project_root / "analysis" / "AmesHousing-working.csv"
    year_columns = ["Year Built", "Year Remod/Add", "Garage Yr Blt", "Yr Sold"]
    homes_df = pd.read_csv(
        housing_path,
        dtype={column: "string" for column in year_columns},
    )
    
    print(f"Loaded {homes_df.shape[0]:,} homes and {homes_df.shape[1]} columns.")
    outputs=1, execution=2
    --- 3 markdown id=db78efb8 ---
    ## Numeric summary statistics
    
    Year columns are strings, so they are excluded from this numeric summary.
    --- 4 code id=f7b20e21 ---
    numeric_summary = homes_df.describe().T
    display(numeric_summary)
    outputs=1, execution=3
    --- 5 markdown id=sales-year-visualizations-heading ---
    ## Sales by year
    
    Compare the average sale price with the number of recorded sales for each sale year.
    --- 6 code id=sales-year-summary ---
    sales_by_year = (
        homes_df.groupby("Yr Sold")["SalePrice"]
        .agg(AverageSalePrice="mean", NumberOfSales="count")
        .sort_index()
    )
    display(sales_by_year)
    outputs=1, execution=4
    --- 7 code id=sales-year-plots ---
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    from matplotlib.ticker import StrMethodFormatter
    
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(12, 4))
    
    axes[0].plot(
        sales_by_year.index,
        sales_by_year["AverageSalePrice"],
        marker="o",
        color="steelblue",
    )
    axes[0].set_title("Average Sale Price by Year")
    axes[0].set_xlabel("Year Sold")
    axes[0].set_ylabel("Average Sale Price")
    axes[0].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}"))
    axes[0].grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
    
    bars = axes[1].bar(sales_by_year.index, sales_by_year["NumberOfSales"], color="darkseagreen")
    axes[1].set_title("Number of Sales by Year")
    axes[1].set_xlabel("Year Sold")
    axes[1].set_ylabel("Number of Sales")
    axes[1].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
    for bar, count in zip(bars, sales_by_year["NumberOfS
    outputs=1, execution=5
    --- 8 code id=44ae4950 ---
    # Load the expanded seven-county tornado dataset. Keep the year column as strings.
    tornado_path = project_root / "analysis" / "Iowa-central-counties-tornadoes-working.csv"
    tornadoes_df = pd.read_csv(tornado_path, dtype={"Year": "string"})
    
    years_of_interest = {str(year) for year in range(2005, 2011)}
    tornadoes_2005_2010_df = tornadoes_df.loc[
        tornadoes_df["Year"].isin(years_of_interest)
    ].copy()
    
    print(f"Loaded {len(tornadoes_df):,} tornado records across all years.")
    print(f"Records from 2005 to 2010: {len(tornadoes_2005_2010_df):,}")
    print("Years in the selected data:")
    display(tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Year"].value_counts().sort_index().to_frame("Tornado Count"))
    outputs=2, execution=6
    --- 9 markdown id=tornado-summary-heading ---
    ## Tornado summary statistics, 2005 to 2010
    
    These summaries use only tornado records in the selected years. County counts and intensity counts show how the records are distributed within that period.
    --- 10 code id=tornado-county-counts ---
    tornado_count_by_county = (
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df.groupby("County")["EventID"]
        .nunique()
        .sort_values(ascending=False)
        .rename("Tornado Count")
        .to_frame()
    )
    display(tornado_count_by_county)
    
    display(
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Intensity"]
        .value_counts(dropna=False)
        .rename_axis("Intensity")
        .to_frame("Tornado Count")
    )
    outputs=2, execution=7
    --- 11 code id=eb20f426-0797-4307-875b-81c03e90b87e ---
    impact_columns = [
        "PropertyDamageUSD", "CropDamageUSD",
        "TornadoLengthMiles", "TornadoWidthYards",
    ]
    tornado_impact_summary = tornadoes_2005_2010_df[impact_columns].describe().T
    display(tornado_impact_summary)
    outputs=1, execution=8

    Compare the average sale price with the number of recorded sales for each sale year.
    === sales-year-summary ===
    sales_by_year = (
    
        homes_df.groupby("Yr Sold")["SalePrice"]

        .sort_index()

    display(sales_by_year)
    === sales-year-plots ===
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    
    from matplotlib.ticker import StrMethodFormatter
    
    
    
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(12, 4))
    
    
    
    axes[0].plot(
    
        sales_by_year.index,
    
        sales_by_year["AverageSalePrice"],
    
        marker="o",
    
        color="steelblue",
    
    )
    
    axes[0].set_title("Average Sale Price by Year")
    
    axes[0].set_xlabel("Year Sold")
    
    axes[0].set_ylabel("Average Sale Price")
    
    axes[0].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}"))
    
    axes[0].grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
    
    
    
    bars = axes[1].bar(sales_by_year.index, sales_by_year["NumberOfSales"], color="darkseagreen")
    
    axes[1].set_title("Number of Sales by Year")
    
    axes[1].set_xlabel("Year Sold")
    
    axes[1].set_ylabel("Number of Sales")
    
    axes[1].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
    
    for bar, count in zip(bars, sales_by_year["NumberOfSales"]):
    
        axes[1].annotate(f"{count:,.0f}", (bar.get_x() + bar.get_width() / 2, bar.get_height()),
    
                         xytext=(0, 3), textcoords="offset points", ha="center")
    
    
    
    fig.tight_layout()
    
    plt.show()

    $ $p=(Resolve-Path -LiteralPath 'analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb').Path; $nb=[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($p,[System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8) | ConvertFrom-Json; $edited=@('sales-year-summary','sales-year-plots'); foreach($c in $nb.cells){if($c.id -in $edited){$c.PSObject.Properties['execution_count'].Value=$null;$c.PSObject.Properties['outputs'].Value=@()}}; $json=ConvertTo-Json -InputObject $nb -Depth 100; [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($p,$json,[System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)); $check=[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText($p,[System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8) | ConvertFrom-Json; foreach($id in $edited){$c=$check.cells | Where-Object id -eq $id; "--$id--"; $c.source -join ''; "outputs=$($c.outputs.Count); execution_count=$($c.execution_count)"}; "JSON valid; cells=$($check.cells.Count)"
    --sales-year-summary--
    sales_by_time = (
        homes_df.assign(
            SaleMonth=pd.to_datetime(
                homes_df["Yr Sold"] + "-"
                + homes_df["Mo Sold"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                format="%Y-%m-%d",
                errors="coerce",
            )
        )
        .groupby("SaleMonth")
        .agg(
            AverageSalePrice=("SalePrice", "mean"),
            NumberOfSales=("SalePrice", "count"),
        )
        .sort_index()
        .asfreq("MS")
    )
    sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"] = sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"].fillna(0).astype("int64")
    print(f"Monthly series: {sales_by_time.index.min():%b %Y} to {sales_by_time.index.max():%b %Y}")
    display(sales_by_time)
    outputs=0; execution_count=
    --sales-year-plots--
    import matplotlib.dates as mdates
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    from matplotlib.ticker import StrMethodFormatter
    
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 1, figsize=(12, 7), sharex=True)
    
    axes[0].plot(
        sales_by_time.index,
        sales_by_time["AverageSalePrice"],
        marker="o",
        markersize=3,
        color="steelblue",
    )
    axes[0].set_title("Average Sale Price per Month")
    axes[0].set_ylabel("Average Sale Price")
    axes[0].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}"))
    axes[0].grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
    
    axes[1].plot(
        sales_by_time.index,
        sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"],
        marker="o",
        markersize=3,
        color="darkseagreen",
    )
    axes[1].set_title("Number of Sales per Month")
    axes[1].set_xlabel("Sale Month")
    axes[1].set_ylabel("Number of Sales")
    axes[1].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
    axes[1].xaxis.set_major_locator(mdates.MonthLocator(interval=6))
    axes[1].xaxis.set_major_formatter(mdates.DateFormatter("%b %Y"))
    for ax in axes:
        ax.grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
        ax.tick_params(axis="x", rotation=45)
    fig.tight_layout()
    plt.show()
    outputs=0; execution_count=

    # Load the expanded seven-county tornado dataset. Keep the year column as strings.
    tornado_path = project_root / "analysis" / "Iowa-central-counties-tornadoes-working.csv"
    tornadoes_df = pd.read_csv(tornado_path, dtype={"Year": "string"})
    tornadoes_df["Mo"] = pd.to_datetime(tornadoes_df["EventDateTime"], errors="coerce").dt.month.astype("int64")
    
    years_of_interest = {str(year) for year in range(2005, 2011)}
    tornadoes_2005_2010_df = tornadoes_df.loc[
        tornadoes_df["Year"].isin(years_of_interest)
    ].copy()
    
    print(f"Loaded {len(tornadoes_df):,} tornado records across all years.")
    print(f"Records from 2005 to 2010: {len(tornadoes_2005_2010_df):,}")
    print("Tornado counts by month in the selected data:")
    display(tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].value_counts().sort_index().to_frame("Tornado Count"))

## Assistant

I found the cause: the month conversion runs on the full tornado table, and 86 historical `EventDateTime` values use formats that `to_datetime(..., errors="coerce")` doesn’t parse. Those become `NaT`, so `.astype("int64")` fails. I’ll derive `Mo` from the existing month-name column instead; that avoids parsing mixed-format dates.

    $ $n = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath 'analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb' | ConvertFrom-Json; $n.cells | Where-Object { $_.cell_type -eq 'code' -and $_.id -in @('44ae4950','tornado-county-counts','eb20f426-0797-4307-875b-81c03e90b87e','8bb8b30e-3b8d-4227-9c07-c6480c27fce8') } | ForEach-Object { '--- '+$_.id; $_.source -join '' }; Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath 'log' -Force | Select-Object Name,Length
    --- 44ae4950
    # Load the expanded seven-county tornado dataset. Keep the year column as strings.
    tornado_path = project_root / "analysis" / "Iowa-central-counties-tornadoes-working.csv"
    tornadoes_df = pd.read_csv(tornado_path, dtype={"Year": "string"})
    month_numbers = {
        "January": 1, "February": 2, "March": 3, "April": 4,
        "May": 5, "June": 6, "July": 7, "August": 8,
        "September": 9, "October": 10, "November": 11, "December": 12,
    }
    tornadoes_df["Mo"] = tornadoes_df["Month"].map(month_numbers).astype("int64")
    
    years_of_interest = {str(year) for year in range(2005, 2011)}
    tornadoes_2005_2010_df = tornadoes_df.loc[
        tornadoes_df["Year"].isin(years_of_interest)
    ].copy()
    
    print(f"Loaded {len(tornadoes_df):,} tornado records across all years.")
    print(f"Records from 2005 to 2010: {len(tornadoes_2005_2010_df):,}")
    print("Tornado counts by month in the selected data:")
    display(tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].value_counts().sort_index().to_frame("Tornado Count"))
    --- tornado-county-counts
    tornado_count_by_county = (
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df.groupby("County")["EventID"]
        .nunique()
        .sort_values(ascending=False)
        .rename("Tornado Count")
        .to_frame()
    )
    display(tornado_count_by_county)
    
    tornado_count_by_month = (
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df.groupby("Mo")["EventID"]
        .nunique()
        .reindex(range(1, 13), fill_value=0)
        .rename("Tornado Count")
        .to_frame()
    )
    display(tornado_count_by_month)
    
    display(
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Intensity"]
        .value_counts(dropna=False)
        .rename_axis("Intensity")
        .to_frame("Tornado Count")
    )
    --- eb20f426-0797-4307-875b-81c03e90b87e
    impact_columns = [
        "PropertyDamageUSD", "CropDamageUSD",
        "TornadoLengthMiles", "TornadoWidthYards",
    ]
    tornado_impact_summary = tornadoes_2005_2010_df[impact_columns].describe().T
    display(tornado_impact_summary)

## Assistant

The `IntCastingNaNError` came from converting mixed-format `EventDateTime` values: unparsed dates became missing values, and plain `int64` cannot hold them.

I updated the tornado cell to derive numeric months from the existing `Month` names and cleared the outputs for that cell and its dependent tornado summaries. The notebook still parses as valid JSON. Re-run those cells in order to refresh the results: [ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb](</E:/GitHub/coyne-week-7-codex-hands-on/analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb>).

## Activity

    $ $n = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath 'analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb' | ConvertFrom-Json; $n.cells | Where-Object { $_.id -in @('sales-year-summary','sales-year-plots','44ae4950') } | ForEach-Object { '---'+$_.id; $_.source -join '' };
    ---sales-year-summary
    sales_by_time = (
        homes_df.assign(
            SaleMonth=pd.to_datetime(
                homes_df["Yr Sold"] + "-"
                + homes_df["Mo Sold"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                format="%Y-%m-%d",
                errors="coerce",
            )
        )
        .groupby("SaleMonth")
        .agg(
            AverageSalePrice=("SalePrice", "mean"),
            NumberOfSales=("SalePrice", "count"),
        )
        .sort_index()
        .asfreq("MS")
    )
    sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"] = sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"].fillna(0).astype("int64")
    print(f"Monthly series: {sales_by_time.index.min():%b %Y} to {sales_by_time.index.max():%b %Y}")
    display(sales_by_time)
    ---sales-year-plots
    import matplotlib.dates as mdates
    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    from matplotlib.ticker import StrMethodFormatter
    
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 1, figsize=(12, 7), sharex=True)
    
    axes[0].plot(
        sales_by_time.index,
        sales_by_time["AverageSalePrice"],
        marker="o",
        markersize=3,
        color="steelblue",
    )
    axes[0].set_title("Average Sale Price per Month")
    axes[0].set_ylabel("Average Sale Price")
    axes[0].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}"))
    axes[0].grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
    
    axes[1].plot(
        sales_by_time.index,
        sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"],
        marker="o",
        markersize=3,
        color="darkseagreen",
    )
    axes[1].set_title("Number of Sales per Month")
    axes[1].set_xlabel("Sale Month")
    axes[1].set_ylabel("Number of Sales")
    axes[1].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
    axes[1].xaxis.set_major_locator(mdates.MonthLocator(interval=6))
    axes[1].xaxis.set_major_formatter(mdates.DateFormatter("%b %Y"))
    for ax in axes:
        ax.grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
        ax.tick_params(axis="x", rotation=45)
    fig.tight_layout()
    plt.show()

    $ $path = 'analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb'; $nb = Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $path | ConvertFrom-Json; $anchor = -1; for ($i=0; $i -lt $nb.cells.Count; $i++) { if ($nb.cells[$i].id -eq 'eb20f426-0797-4307-875b-81c03e90b87e') { $anchor=$i; break } }; if ($anchor -lt 0) { throw 'Could not find tornado impact summary cell.' }; $md = @'
        ## Monthly tornado frequency time series
        
        Count tornadoes chronologically by year-month for 2005 through 2010, filling months with no tornadoes as zero.
        '@; $code = @'
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df = tornadoes_2005_2010_df.assign(
            EventMonth=pd.to_datetime(
                tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Year"] + "-"
                + tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                format="%Y-%m-%d",
                errors="coerce",
            )
        )
        
        tornado_frequency_by_time = (
            tornadoes_2005_2010_df.groupby("EventMonth")["EventID"]
            .nunique()
            .rename("Tornado Count")
            .to_frame()
            .sort_index()
            .asfreq("MS", fill_value=0)
        )
        tornado_frequency_by_time["Tornado Count"] = (
            tornado_frequency_by_time["Tornado Count"].fillna(0).astype("int64")
        )
        print(
            f"Monthly series: {tornado_frequency_by_time.index.min():%b %Y} "
            f"to {tornado_frequency_by_time.index.max():%b %Y}"
        )
        display(tornado_frequency_by_time)
        '@; $plot = @'
        import matplotlib.dates as mdates
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
        from matplotlib.ticker import StrMethodFormatter
        
        fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(12, 4))
        ax.plot(
            tornado_frequency_by_time.index,
            tornado_frequency_by_time["Tornado Count"],
            marker="o",
            markersize=4,
            color="firebrick",
        )
        ax.set_title("Tornadoes per Month, 2005–2010")
        ax.set_xlabel("Month")
        ax.set_ylabel("Tornado Count")
        ax.yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
        ax.xaxis.set_major_locator(mdates.MonthLocator(interval=6))
        ax.xaxis.set_major_formatter(mdates.DateFormatter("%b %Y"))
        ax.grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
        ax.tick_params(axis="x", rotation=45)
        fig.tight_layout()
        plt.show()

    $ $path='analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb'; $nb=Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $path|ConvertFrom-Json; $anchor=-1; for($i=0;$i -lt $nb.cells.Count;$i++){if($nb.cells[$i].id -eq 'tornado-monthly-timeseries-plot'){$anchor=$i;break}}; if($anchor -lt 0){throw 'Could not find tornado time-series plot cell.'}; $md=@'
        ## Monthly housing and tornado comparison
        
        The three panels share one monthly timeline from 2005 through 2010. Housing values are left blank for 2005, and each panel keeps its own scale so the different measures remain readable.
        '@; $code=@'
        comparison_months = pd.date_range("2005-01-01", "2010-12-01", freq="MS")
        comparison_by_time = pd.DataFrame(index=comparison_months)
        comparison_by_time["AverageSalePrice"] = sales_by_time["AverageSalePrice"].reindex(comparison_months)
        comparison_by_time["NumberOfSales"] = sales_by_time["NumberOfSales"].reindex(comparison_months)
        comparison_by_time["TornadoCount"] = tornado_frequency_by_time["Tornado Count"].reindex(
            comparison_months, fill_value=0
        )
        comparison_by_time.loc[
            comparison_by_time.index.year == 2005,
            ["AverageSalePrice", "NumberOfSales"],
        ] = float("nan")
        
        display(comparison_by_time)
        '@; $plot=@'
        import matplotlib.dates as mdates
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
        from matplotlib.ticker import StrMethodFormatter
        
        fig, axes = plt.subplots(3, 1, figsize=(13, 10), sharex=True)
        axes[0].plot(
            comparison_by_time.index,
            comparison_by_time["AverageSalePrice"],
            marker="o",
            markersize=3,
            color="steelblue",
        )
        axes[0].set_title("Average Sale Price per Month")
        axes[0].set_ylabel("Sale Price")
        axes[0].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("${x:,.0f}"))
        
        axes[1].plot(
            comparison_by_time.index,
            comparison_by_time["NumberOfSales"],
            marker="o",
            markersize=3,
            color="darkseagreen",
        )
        axes[1].set_title("Number of Home Sales per Month")
        axes[1].set_ylabel("Home Sales")
        axes[1].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
        
        axes[2].plot(
            comparison_by_time.index,
            comparison_by_time["TornadoCount"],
            marker="o",
            markersize=4,
            color="firebrick",
        )
        axes[2].set_title("Tornadoes per Month")
        axes[2].set_xlabel("Month")
        axes[2].set_ylabel("Tornadoes")
        axes[2].yaxis.set_major_formatter(StrMethodFormatter("{x:,.0f}"))
        axes[2].xaxis.set_major_locator(mdates.MonthLocator(interval=6))
        axes[2].xaxis.set_major_formatter(mdates.DateFormatter("%b %Y"))
        
        for ax in axes:
            ax.set_xlim(comparison_months.min(), comparison_months.max())
            ax.grid(axis="y", alpha=0.3)
            ax.tick_params(axis="x", rotation=45)
        fig.tight_layout()
        plt.show()

## Activity

    $ $p='analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb'; $nb=Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $p|ConvertFrom-Json; $anchor=-1; for($i=0;$i -lt $nb.cells.Count;$i++){if($nb.cells[$i].id -eq 'f7b20e21'){$anchor=$i;break}}; if($anchor -lt 0){throw 'Could not find numeric summary cell.'}; $md=@'
        ## Sale price by basement presence
        
        Compare sale prices for homes with and without a basement across the full housing dataset, regardless of sale month. Basement presence is defined as `Total Bsmt SF > 0`. The bootstrap interval estimates uncertainty in the median price difference; this is an unadjusted association and does not isolate a causal basement premium.
        '@; $code=@'
        import numpy as np
        
        basement_homes = homes_df.assign(
            HasBasement=homes_df["Total Bsmt SF"].fillna(0).gt(0)
        )
        with_basement = basement_homes.loc[basement_homes["HasBasement"], "SalePrice"].dropna().to_numpy()
        without_basement = basement_homes.loc[~basement_homes["HasBasement"], "SalePrice"].dropna().to_numpy()
        
        basement_price_summary = (
            basement_homes.groupby("HasBasement")["SalePrice"]
            .agg(SampleSize="count", MedianSalePrice="median")
            .rename(index={False: "No basement", True: "Basement"})
        )
        display(basement_price_summary)
        
        observed_median_difference = np.median(with_basement) - np.median(without_basement)
        rng = np.random.default_rng(42)
        n_resamples = 10_000
        batch_size = 250
        bootstrap_differences = np.empty(n_resamples)
        for start in range(0, n_resamples, batch_size):
            batch_n = min(batch_size, n_resamples - start)
            with_samples = with_basement[
                rng.integers(0, len(with_basement), size=(batch_n, len(with_basement)))
            ]
            without_samples = without_basement[
                rng.integers(0, len(without_basement), size=(batch_n, len(without_basement)))
            ]
            bootstrap_differences[start:start + batch_n] = (
                np.median(with_samples, axis=1) - np.median(without_samples, axis=1)
            )
        
        ci_low, ci_high = np.percentile(bootstrap_differences, [2.5, 97.5])
        median_difference_result = pd.DataFrame(
            {
                "Estimate": [observed_median_difference],
                "95% Bootstrap CI Lower": [ci_low],
                "95% Bootstrap CI Upper": [ci_high],
                "CI Excludes Zero": [ci_low > 0 or ci_high < 0],
            },
            index=["Median price: basement minus no basement"],
        )
        display(median_difference_result)
        print(
            "The 95% bootstrap interval excludes zero: "
            f"{ci_low > 0 or ci_high < 0}."
        )
        '@; $cells=@([pscustomobject]@{cell_type='markdown';id='basement-price-heading';metadata=[pscustomobject]@{};source=@($md)},[pscustomobject]@{cell_type='code';execution_count=$null;id='basement-price-comparison';metadata=[pscustomobject]@{};outputs=@();source=@($code)}); $before=@($nb.cells[0..$anchor]); $after=@(); if($anchor+1 -lt $nb.cells.Count){$after=@($nb.cells[($anchor+1)..($nb.cells.Count-1)])}; $nb.cells=@($before)+$cells+@($after); $json=ConvertTo-Json -InputObject $nb -Depth 100; [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText((Resolve-Path $p),$json,[System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)); $entry=@'

    $ $p='analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb'; $nb=Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $p|ConvertFrom-Json; $ci=$nb.cells|Where-Object id -eq 'basement-price-comparison'; $ciSource=$ci.source -join ''; $old=@'

        '@; if(-not $ciSource.Contains($old)){throw 'Could not find existing confidence interval printout.'}; $ci.source=@($ciSource.Replace($old,$new)); $ci.outputs=@(); $ci.execution_count=$null; $last=-1; for($i=0;$i -lt $nb.cells.Count;$i++){if($nb.cells[$i].id -eq 'monthly-comparison-plot'){$last=$i;break}}; if($last -lt 0){throw 'Could not find comparison plot cell.'}; $md=@'
        ## Basement purchases around tornado months
        
        For each calendar month with at least one tornado in the seven-county dataset (2005-2010), compare Ames home purchases in the month before, the tornado month, and the month after. Multiple tornadoes in the same calendar month are treated as one tornado month. The basement purchase percentage and median sale price difference are descriptive, unadjusted comparisons; event windows can overlap when tornadoes occur in nearby months.
        '@; $code=@'
        # Build one row per Ames sale and mark whether its total basement area is positive.
        home_sales_for_tornado_windows = homes_df.assign(
            SaleMonth=pd.to_datetime(
                homes_df["Yr Sold"] + "-"
                + homes_df["Mo Sold"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                format="%Y-%m-%d",
                errors="coerce",
            ),
            HasBasement=homes_df["Total Bsmt SF"].fillna(0).gt(0),
        ).dropna(subset=["SaleMonth", "SalePrice"])
        
        # Use unique active calendar months so county clusters in one month do not repeat sales.
        tornado_event_months = pd.DatetimeIndex(
            pd.to_datetime(
                tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Year"] + "-"
                + tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                format="%Y-%m-%d",
                errors="coerce",
            )
            .dropna()
            .drop_duplicates()
            .sort_values()
        )
        
        window_specs = [(-1, "Month before"), (0, "Tornado month"), (1, "Month after")]
        window_sales = []
        for offset, window_name in window_specs:
            for tornado_month in tornado_event_months:
                sale_month = tornado_month + pd.DateOffset(months=offset)
                matched_sales = home_sales_for_tornado_windows.loc[
                    home_sales_for_tornado_windows["SaleMonth"].eq(sale_month)
                ].copy()
                if not matched_sales.empty:
                    matched_sales["Window"] = window_name
                    matched_sales["TornadoEventMonth"] = tornado_month
                    window_sales.append(matched_sales)
        
        if not window_sales:
            raise ValueError("No Ames home sales matched the tornado event windows.")
        
        tornado_window_sales = pd.concat(window_sales, ignore_index=True)
        window_summary_rows = []
        for _, window_name in window_specs:
            window_data = tornado_window_sales.loc[tornado_window_sales["Window"].eq(window_name)]
            basement_prices = window_data.loc[window_data["HasBasement"], "SalePrice"]
            no_basement_prices = window_data.loc[~window_data["HasBasement"], "SalePrice"]
            median_with_basement = basement_prices.median()
            median_without_basement = no_basement_prices.median()
            window_summary_rows.append(
                {
                    "Window": window_name,
                    "Tornado Months Matched": window_data["TornadoEventMonth"].nunique(),
                    "Home Purchases": len(window_data),
                    "Basement Purchases": int(window_data["HasBasement"].sum()),
                    "Purchases with Basement (%)": 100 * window_data["HasBasement"].mean(),
                    "Median Price with Basement": median_with_basement,
                    "Median Price without Basement": median_without_basement,
                    "Median Basement Price Difference": median_with_basement - median_without_basement,
                }
            )
        
        tornado_window_summary = pd.DataFrame(window_summary_rows).set_index("Window")
        display(tornado_window_summary)

    import numpy as np
    
    basement_homes = homes_df.assign(
        HasBasement=homes_df["Total Bsmt SF"].fillna(0).gt(0)
    )
    with_basement = basement_homes.loc[basement_homes["HasBasement"], "SalePrice"].dropna().to_numpy()
    without_basement = basement_homes.loc[~basement_homes["HasBasement"], "SalePrice"].dropna().to_numpy()
    
    basement_price_summary = (
        basement_homes.groupby("HasBasement")["SalePrice"]
        .agg(SampleSize="count", MedianSalePrice="median")
        .rename(index={False: "No basement", True: "Basement"})
    )
    display(basement_price_summary)
    
    observed_median_difference = np.median(with_basement) - np.median(without_basement)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(42)
    n_resamples = 10_000
    batch_size = 250
    bootstrap_differences = np.empty(n_resamples)
    for start in range(0, n_resamples, batch_size):
        batch_n = min(batch_size, n_resamples - start)
        with_samples = with_basement[
            rng.integers(0, len(with_basement), size=(batch_n, len(with_basement)))
        ]
        without_samples = without_basement[
            rng.integers(0, len(without_basement), size=(batch_n, len(without_basement)))
        ]
        bootstrap_differences[start:start + batch_n] = (
            np.median(with_samples, axis=1) - np.median(without_samples, axis=1)
        )
    
    ci_low, ci_high = np.percentile(bootstrap_differences, [2.5, 97.5])
    median_difference_result = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "Estimate": [observed_median_difference],
            "95% Bootstrap CI Lower": [ci_low],

        ## Tornado counts and purchases without a basement

        '@; $summary=@'
        # Aggregate unique tornado events by calendar month across the selected counties.
        tornado_monthly_counts_for_mix = (
            tornadoes_2005_2010_df.assign(
                EventMonth=pd.to_datetime(
                    tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Year"] + "-"
                    + tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                    format="%Y-%m-%d",
                    errors="coerce",
                )
            )
            .groupby("EventMonth")["EventID"]
            .nunique()
            .rename("Tornado Count")
        )
        
        monthly_home_mix = (
            home_sales_for_tornado_windows.groupby("SaleMonth")
            .agg(
                HomePurchases=("SalePrice", "count"),
                PurchasesWithoutBasement=("HasBasement", lambda values: (~values).sum()),
            )
        )
        comparison_months = pd.date_range("2005-01-01", "2010-12-01", freq="MS")
        tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month = pd.DataFrame(index=comparison_months)
        tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Tornado Count"] = (
            tornado_monthly_counts_for_mix.reindex(comparison_months, fill_value=0).astype("int64")
        )
        tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Home Purchases"] = (
            monthly_home_mix["HomePurchases"].reindex(comparison_months).astype("Int64")
        )
        tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Purchases without Basement"] = (
            monthly_home_mix["PurchasesWithoutBasement"].reindex(comparison_months).astype("Int64")
        )
        tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Purchases without Basement (%)"] = (
            100 * tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Purchases without Basement"]
            / tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Home Purchases"]
        )
        tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Tornado Activity"] = (
            tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month["Tornado Count"]
            .gt(0)
            .map({True: "Tornado month", False: "No tornado"})
        )
        display(tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month)
        
        observed_months = tornado_vs_no_basement_by_month.dropna(
            subset=["Purchases without Basement (%)"]
        )
        activity_summary_rows = []
        for activity in ["No tornado", "Tornado month"]:
            activity_months = observed_months.loc[observed_months["Tornado Activity"].eq(activity)]
            total_purchases = int(activity_months["Home Purchases"].sum())
            no_basement_purchases = int(activity_months["Purchases without Basement"].sum())
            activity_summary_rows.append(
                {
                    "Tornado Activity": activity,
                    "Months": len(activity_months),
                    "Tornadoes": int(activity_months["Tornado Count"].sum()),
                    "Home Purchases": total_purchases,
                    "Purchases without Basement": no_basement_purchases,
                    "Pooled Purchases without Basement (%)": (
                        100 * no_basement_purchases / total_purchases if total_purchases else float("nan")
                    ),
                    "Mean Monthly without-Basement (%)": activity_months[
                        "Purchases without Basement (%)"
                    ].mean(),
                }
            )
        tornado_activity_mix_summary = pd.DataFrame(activity_summary_rows).set_index("Tornado Activity")
        display(tornado_activity_mix_summary)
        '@; $plot=@'

## User

Alright I still am not convinced that there's a noticeable difference between basement and non basement homes, but to be sure let's wrap up our analysis with two things. we have the set of 9 tornado months and 46 others, can we fit a logistic regression with both the basement/no basement sales, and tornado/no tornado months? if you need to amend the data in any way to make the figures 0/1 you can do that whichever way you choose. the second item that might be worth looking at is an anova for both the tornado and non/tornado months. If there's no significant difference in either of those i think we can safely say that the Iowans do not appear to allow the threat of tornadoes to impact their home-purchasing decisions

## Assistant

I’ll model basement presence as a 0/1 outcome and tornado-active month as a 0/1 predictor, using the 9 active and 46 inactive sale months you identified. For sale prices, I’ll use a two-factor ANOVA-style model for basement status, tornado status, and their interaction. I’ll account for repeated sales within the same month and control for calendar month and year, since tornado activity is seasonal.

## Activity

    $ $p='analysis/ames_tornado_exploration.ipynb'; $nb=Get-Content -Raw -LiteralPath $p|ConvertFrom-Json; $anchor=-1; for($i=0;$i -lt $nb.cells.Count;$i++){if($nb.cells[$i].id -eq 'tornado-no-basement-plots'){$anchor=$i;break}}; if($anchor -lt 0){throw 'Could not find the latest tornado comparison plot.'}; $md=@'
        ## Final models: basement purchases and tornado months
        
        Use the housing sale months that overlap the 2005-2010 tornado data. `HasBasement01` is 1 when `Total Bsmt SF > 0`; `TornadoMonth01` is 1 when at least one tornado was recorded in the seven-county region that calendar month. The logistic model estimates whether basement purchases are more likely in tornado months. The factorial sale-price model estimates basement, tornado-month, and interaction effects. Both models control for calendar month and sale year and use month-clustered standard errors because tornado status is shared by all sales in a month.
        
        These are observational comparisons. The price model does not control for home size, quality, neighborhood, or other property differences, so its coefficients cannot be read as a causal willingness-to-pay effect. A non-significant result would mean no association was detected with this sample and model; it would not prove tornado risk has no effect.
        '@; $code=@'
        import numpy as np
        import statsmodels.formula.api as smf
        
        # Rebuild the sale-month dataset and binary indicators for the two models.
        model_homes = homes_df.assign(
            SaleMonth=pd.to_datetime(
                homes_df["Yr Sold"] + "-"
                + homes_df["Mo Sold"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
                format="%Y-%m-%d",
                errors="coerce",
            ),
            HasBasement01=homes_df["Total Bsmt SF"].fillna(0).gt(0).astype("int64"),
        ).dropna(subset=["SaleMonth", "SalePrice"])
        
        model_tornado_months = pd.to_datetime(
            tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Year"] + "-"
            + tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
            format="%Y-%m-%d",
            errors="coerce",
        )
        model_tornado_counts = (
            tornadoes_2005_2010_df.assign(EventMonth=model_tornado_months)
            .dropna(subset=["EventMonth"])
            .groupby("EventMonth")["EventID"]
            .nunique()
        )
        
        model_homes["TornadoCount"] = model_homes["SaleMonth"].map(model_tornado_counts).fillna(0).astype("int64")
        model_homes["TornadoMonth01"] = model_homes["TornadoCount"].gt(0).astype("int64")
        model_homes["SaleYear"] = model_homes["SaleMonth"].dt.year.astype("string")
        model_homes["SaleCalendarMonth"] = model_homes["SaleMonth"].dt.month.astype("string")
        
        month_status = model_homes[["SaleMonth", "TornadoMonth01"]].drop_duplicates()
        month_status_counts = (
            month_status["TornadoMonth01"]
            .value_counts()
            .reindex([0, 1], fill_value=0)
            .rename(index={0: "No tornado", 1: "Tornado month"})
            .rename("Sale Months")
            .to_frame()
        )
        display(month_status_counts)
        print(
            f"Sale months: {int(month_status_counts.loc['Tornado month', 'Sale Months'])} tornado months, "
            f"{int(month_status_counts.loc['No tornado', 'Sale Months'])} non-tornado months."
        )
        
        basement_by_tornado = pd.crosstab(
            model_homes["TornadoMonth01"].map({0: "No tornado", 1: "Tornado month"}),
            model_homes["HasBasement01"].map({0: "No basement", 1: "Basement"}),
        ).reindex(index=["No tornado", "Tornado month"], columns=["No basement", "Basement"], fill_value=0)
        display(basement_by_tornado)
        
        # Logistic regression: odds of a purchased home having a basement.
        # Cluster the standard errors by sale month to account for shared monthly exposure.
        logit_fit = smf.logit(
            "HasBasement01 ~ TornadoMonth01 + C(SaleYear) + C(SaleCalendarMonth)",
            data=model_homes,
        ).fit(
            disp=False,
            cov_type="cluster",
            cov_kwds={"groups": model_homes["SaleMonth"], "use_correction": True},
        )
        logit_ci = logit_fit.conf_int().loc["TornadoMonth01"]
        logit_result = pd.DataFrame(
            {
                "Odds Ratio (tornado vs no tornado month)": [np.exp(logit_fit.params["TornadoMonth01"])],
                "95% CI Lower": [np.exp(logit_ci.iloc[0])],
                "95% CI Upper": [np.exp(logit_ci.iloc[1])],
                "Cluster-robust p-value": [logit_fit.pvalues["TornadoMonth01"]],
            },
            index=["Basement purchase"],
        )
        display(logit_result)
        
        # Factorial ANOVA-style model for sale price with month-clustered term tests.
        price_fit = smf.ols(
            "SalePrice ~ HasBasement01 * TornadoMonth01 + C(SaleYear) + C(SaleCalendarMonth)",
            data=model_homes,
        ).fit(
            cov_type="cluster",
            cov_kwds={"groups": model_homes["SaleMonth"], "use_correction": True},
        )
        price_term_tests = price_fit.wald_test_terms(skip_single=False).table
        terms_of_interest = [
            "HasBasement01",
            "TornadoMonth01",
            "HasBasement01:TornadoMonth01",
        ]
        display(price_term_tests.loc[terms_of_interest])
        print("The price-model terms test basement status, tornado-month status, and their interaction.")
        '@; $new=@([pscustomobject]@{cell_type='markdown';id='final-models-heading';metadata=[pscustomobject]@{};source=@($md)},[pscustomobject]@{cell_type='code';execution_count=$null;id='final-logistic-anova-models';metadata=[pscustomobject]@{};outputs=@();source=@($code)}); $before=@($nb.cells[0..$anchor]); $after=@(); if($anchor+1 -lt $nb.cells.Count){$after=@($nb.cells[($anchor+1)..($nb.cells.Count-1)])}; $nb.cells=@($before)+$new+@($after); $json=ConvertTo-Json -InputObject $nb -Depth 100; [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText((Resolve-Path $p),$json,[System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)); $entry=@'

            homes_df["Yr Sold"] + "-"
            + homes_df["Mo Sold"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
            format="%Y-%m-%d",
            errors="coerce",
        ),
        HasBasement01=homes_df["Total Bsmt SF"].fillna(0).gt(0).astype("int64"),
    ).dropna(subset=["SaleMonth", "SalePrice"])
    
    model_tornado_months = pd.to_datetime(
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Year"] + "-"
        + tornadoes_2005_2010_df["Mo"].astype("string").str.zfill(2) + "-01",
        format="%Y-%m-%d",
        errors="coerce",
    )
    model_tornado_counts = (
        tornadoes_2005_2010_df.assign(EventMonth=model_tornado_months)
        .dropna(subset=["EventMonth"])
        .groupby("EventMonth")["EventID"]
        .nunique()
    )
    
    model_homes["TornadoCount"] = model_homes["SaleMonth"].map(model_tornado_counts).fillna(0).astype("int64")
    model_homes["TornadoMonth01"] = model_homes["TornadoCount"].gt(0).astype("int64")
    model_homes["SaleYear"] = model_homes["SaleMonth"].dt.year.astype("string")
    model_homes["SaleCalendarMonth"] = model_homes["SaleMonth"].dt.month.astype("string")
    
    month_status = model_homes[["SaleMonth", "TornadoMonth01"]].drop_duplicates()
    month_status_counts = (
        month_status["TornadoMonth01"]
        .value_counts()
        .reindex([0, 1], fill_value=0)
        .rename(index={0: "No tornado", 1: "Tornado month"})
        .rename("Sale Months")
        .to_frame()
    )
    display(month_status_counts)
    print(
        f"Sale months: {int(month_status_counts.loc['Tornado month', 'Sale Months'])} tornado months, "
        f"{int(month_status_counts.loc['No tornado', 'Sale Months'])} non-tornado months."
    )
    
    basement_by_tornado = pd.crosstab(
        model_homes["TornadoMonth01"].map({0: "No tornado", 1: "Tornado month"}),
        model_homes["HasBasement01"].map({0: "No basement", 1: "Basement"}),
    ).reindex(index=["No tornado", "Tornado month"], columns=["No basement", "Basement"], fill_value=0)
    display(basement_by_tornado)
    
    # Logistic regression: odds of a purchased home having a basement.
    # Cluster the standard errors by sale month to account for shared monthly exposure.
    logit_fit = smf.logit(
        "HasBasement01 ~ TornadoMonth01 + C(SaleYear) + C(SaleCalendarMonth)",
        data=model_homes,
    ).fit(
        disp=False,
        cov_type="cluster",
        cov_kwds={"groups": model_homes["SaleMonth"], "use_correction": True},
    )
    logit_ci = logit_fit.conf_int().loc["TornadoMonth01"]
    logit_result = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "Odds Ratio (tornado vs no tornado month)": [np.exp(logit_fit.params["TornadoMonth01"])],
            "95% CI Lower": [np.exp(logit_ci.iloc[0])],
            "95% CI Upper": [np.exp(logit_ci.iloc[1])],
            "Cluster-robust p-value": [logit_fit.pvalues["TornadoMonth01"]],
        },
        index=["Basement purchase"],
    )
    logit_result_display = logit_result.copy()
    for column in ["Odds Ratio (tornado vs no tornado month)", "95% CI Lower", "95% CI Upper"]:
        logit_result_display[column] = logit_result_display[column].map(lambda value: f"{value:.3f}")
    logit_result_display["Cluster-robust p-value"] = logit_result_display[
        "Cluster-robust p-value"
    ].map(lambda value: "<0.0001" if value < 0.0001 else f"{value:.4f}")
    display(logit_result_display)
    
    # Factorial ANOVA-style model for sale price with month-clustered term tests.
    price_fit = smf.ols(
        "SalePrice ~ HasBasement01 * TornadoMonth01 + C(SaleYear) + C(SaleCalendarMonth)",
        data=model_homes,
    ).fit(
        cov_type="cluster",
        cov_kwds={"groups": model_homes["SaleMonth"], "use_correction": True},
    )
    price_term_tests = price_fit.wald_test_terms(skip_single=False).table
    terms_of_interest = [
        "HasBasement01",
        "TornadoMonth01",
        "HasBasement01:TornadoMonth01",
    ]
    price_term_tests_display = price_term_tests.loc[terms_of_interest].copy()
    for column in ["statistic", "pvalue"]:
        price_term_tests_display[column] = price_term_tests_display[column].map(
            lambda value: (
                "<0.0001"
                if column == "pvalue" and float(np.asarray(value).squeeze()) < 0.0001
                else f"{float(np.asarray(value).squeeze()):.4f}"
                if column == "pvalue"
                else f"{float(np.asarray(value).squeeze()):.3f}"
            )
        )
    display(price_term_tests_display)
    print("The price-model terms test basement status, tornado-month status, and their interaction.")

    ## Final models: basement purchases and tornado months
    
    Use the housing sale months that overlap the 2005-2010 tornado data. `HasBasement01` is 1 when `Total Bsmt SF > 0`; `TornadoMonth01` is 1 when at least one tornado was recorded in the seven-county region that calendar month. The logistic model estimates whether basement purchases are more likely in tornado months. The factorial sale-price model estimates basement, tornado-month, and interaction effects. Both models control for calendar month and sale year and use month-clustered standard errors because tornado status is shared by all sales in a month. With only 9 tornado-active sale months, cluster-robust p-values are approximate and the tests may have limited power.
    
    These are observational comparisons. The price model does not control for home size, quality, neighborhood, or other property differences, so its coefficients cannot be read as a causal willingness-to-pay effect. A non-significant result would mean no association was detected with this sample and model; it would not prove tornado risk has no effect.
    
    **AI produced:** Added binary basement and tornado-month indicators; a logistic regression for basement-purchase odds; and a factorial sale-price model testing basement status, tornado-month status, and their interaction. Models control for sale year and calendar month and use sale-month-clustered standard errors. The notebook formats odds ratios and p-values to retain useful precision. It explains that non-significance is not proof of no impact and that unadjusted property attributes limit causal interpretation.

## Assistant

I don’t think another exploratory test is essential before reporting. I’d keep the conclusion narrow: **these analyses did not detect a statistically significant association** between tornado-active months and basement purchases or sale prices. That’s more defensible than saying tornadoes have no impact, especially with only 9 active months.

I’d also describe the matching seasonal patterns as descriptive. Tornadoes and home sales may both be seasonal; that alone doesn’t show that tornado risk changes buying decisions. The basement indicator is only a proxy for safety preferences, and the homes’ size, quality, and neighborhood can also affect price.

One optional robustness check would be a property-adjusted price model, but it isn’t necessary to move to a report.

    $ $reportDir='analysis/reports'; New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path $reportDir | Out-Null; $tex=@'
        \documentclass[11pt]{article}
        \usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}
        \usepackage[T1]{fontenc}
        \usepackage[margin=0.78in]{geometry}
        \usepackage{graphicx}
        \usepackage{booktabs}
